**Texas** ([/ˈtɛksəs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/English) [//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/6/67/En-us-Texas.ogg/En-us-Texas.ogg.mp3](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/6/67/En-us-Texas.ogg/En-us-Texas.ogg.mp3)<sup>ⓘ</sup> [*TEK-səss*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Pronunciation_respelling_key))[^cite_note-12] is a [state](18618239.md) in the United States. Located in the south-central part of the country, it borders [Louisiana](18130.md), [Arkansas](1930.md), [Oklahoma](22489.md), and [New Mexico](21649.md). It also [borders Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexico%E2%80%93United_States_border) along the [Rio Grande](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_Grande), and has a coastline on the [Gulf of Mexico](21076367.md). Covering 268,596 square miles (695,660 km<sup>2</sup>) and with an estimated population of over 31.7 million residents in 2025,[^cite_note-13] it is the [second-largest state by both area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_and_territories_by_area) and [population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_and_territories_by_population). Texas is nicknamed the "**Lone Star State**" for the single star depicted on the [Republic of Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Texas) flag in 1836 during the [Texas war for independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_war_for_independance) from Mexico, symbolizing Texans' enduring independent spirit and distinct national identity.[^cite_note-14] The lone star design was originally flown in 1819 during the [Long Expedition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long_Expedition), an early attempt to liberate Texas from [Spanish imperial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Empire) rule.[^cite_note-15]&#x20;

[Spain](26667.md) was the first European country to [claim and control Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Texas), followed by brief [French colonization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_colonization_of_Texas). The territory became part of [Mexico](3966054.md) after its independence from Spain in 1821. Increasing tensions between settlers and the Mexican government culminated in the [Texas Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Revolution), which included the [Battle of the Alamo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_the_Alamo), and led to the establishment of the independent Republic of Texas in 1836. In 1845, [Texas joined the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_annexation) as the 28th state, which helped spark the [Mexican–American War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican%E2%80%93American_War). Texas remained a [slave state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slave_state) until the [American Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Civil_War), during which it seceded from the [Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_\(American_Civil_War\)) and joined the [Confederate States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confederate_States_of_America). After the war and the restoration of its representation in the federal government, Texas entered a prolonged period of economic stagnation before undergoing substantial economic growth in the 20th century.&#x20;

The [economy of Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Texas) is highly diversified with major industries including [energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_in_Texas), [agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_Texas), technology, manufacturing, and aerospace. It has the [second-highest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_by_GDP) [gross state product](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gross_state_product) among U.S. states and has led the country [in state export revenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_and_territories_by_exports) since 2002.[^cite_note-Leagueown-16] Its largest metropolitan areas include the [Dallas–Fort Worth metroplex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dallas%E2%80%93Fort_Worth_metroplex) and [Greater Houston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Houston), while [Austin](1998.md) is the state capital. Texas contains diverse [landscapes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Texas) spanning the [Gulf Coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_Coast), [Piney Woods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piney_Woods), plains, hills, deserts, and mountains, reflecting its large size and varied topography.&#x20;

## Etymology

The name *Texas*, based on the [Caddo word](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caddo_language) *táy\:shaʼ* (/tə́jːʃaʔ/) 'friend', was applied, in the spelling *Tejas* or *Texas*,[^cite_note-17][^cite_note-18][^cite_note-19][^cite_note-facts-4] by the Spanish to the [Caddo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caddo) themselves, specifically the [Hasinai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hasinai) Confederacy.[^cite_note-20]&#x20;

During [Spanish colonial rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Texas), in the 18th century, the area was known as *Nuevas Filipinas* ('[New Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Philippines)') and *Nuevo Reino de Filipinas* ('New Kingdom of the Philippines'),[^cite_note-Teja-2010-21] or as *provincia de los Tejas* ('province of the *Tejas*'),[^cite_note-22] later also *provincia de Texas* (or *de Tejas*), ('province of Texas').[^cite_note-23][^cite_note-Teja-2010-21] It was incorporated as *[provincia de Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_Texas)* into the [Mexican Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Mexican_Empire) in 1821, and declared [a republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Texas) in 1836. The [Royal Spanish Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Spanish_Academy) recognizes both spellings, *Tejas* and *Texas*, as Spanish-language forms of the name.[^cite_note-24]&#x20;

The English pronunciation with /ks/ is unetymological, contrary to the historical value of the letter [x](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/X) (/[ʃ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voiceless_postalveolar_fricative)/) in [Spanish orthography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_orthography). Alternative etymologies of the name advanced in the late 19th century connected the name *Texas* with the Spanish word *teja*, meaning 'roof tile', the plural *tejas* being used to designate Indigenous [Pueblo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pueblo) settlements.[^cite_note-Dimitry-25] A 1760s map by [Jacques-Nicolas Bellin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacques-Nicolas_Bellin) shows a village named *Teijas* on the [Trinity River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trinity_River_\(Texas\)), close to the site of modern [Crockett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crockett,_Texas).[^cite_note-Dimitry-25]&#x20;

## History

### Precontact era

Main article: [History of Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Texas)

Further information: [Pre-Columbian Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pre-Columbian_Mexico) and [Native American tribes in Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_American_tribes_in_Texas)



Early Native American tribal territories

Texas lies between two major cultural spheres of [Pre-Columbian North America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pre-Columbian_North_America): the [Southwestern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southwestern_tribes) and the [Plains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plains_Indians) areas. [Archaeologists](18951655.md) have found that three major Indigenous cultures lived in this territory, and reached their developmental peak before the first European contact. These were: the [Ancestral Puebloans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancestral_Puebloans) from the upper Rio Grande region, centered west of Texas; the [Mississippian culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mississippian_culture), also known as [Mound Builders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mound_Builders), which extended along the [Mississippi River Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mississippi_River_Valley) east of Texas; and the civilizations of [Mesoamerica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mesoamerica), which were centered south of Texas. Influence of [Teotihuacan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teotihuacan) in northern Mexico peaked around AD 500 and declined between the 8th and 10th centuries.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERichardsonWintzBoswellAnderson20219-26]&#x20;

When Europeans first arrived in the Texas region, the languages present in the state were [Caddoan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caddoan), [Atakapan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atakapa), [Athabaskan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athabaskan_languages), [Coahuiltecan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coahuiltecan), and [Uto-Aztecan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uto-Aztecan_languages), in addition to several language isolates such as [Tonkawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tonkawa_language). Uto-Aztecan Puebloan and [Jumano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jumanos) peoples lived near the Rio Grande in the western portion of the state, and the Athabaskan-speaking Apache tribes lived throughout the interior. The agricultural, mound-building Caddo controlled much of the northeastern part of the state, along the [Red](194242.md), [Sabine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabine_River_\(Texas%E2%80%93Louisiana\)), [and Neches River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neches_River) basins.[^cite_note-Carter_1995-27][^cite_note-28] Atakapan peoples such as the [Akokisa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akokisa) and [Bidai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bidai) lived along the northeastern Gulf Coast; the [Karankawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karankawa_people) lived along the central coast.[^cite_note-29] At least one tribe of [Coahuiltecans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coahuiltecan), the [Aranama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aranama_people), lived in southern Texas. This entire culture group, primarily centered in northeastern [Mexico](3966054.md), is now extinct.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERichardsonWintzBoswellAnderson202109-30]&#x20;

No one culture was dominant across the territory of present-day Texas, with many peoples inhabiting the area.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERichardsonWintzBoswellAnderson202110-31] Native American tribes who have lived inside the boundaries of present-day Texas include the [Alabama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alabama_people), [Apache](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apache), [Atakapan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atakapa), [Bidai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bidai), [Caddo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caddo), [Aranama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aranama_people), [Comanche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comanche), [Choctaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Choctaw), [Coushatta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coushatta), [Hasinai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hasinai), [Jumano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jumano_people), [Karankawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karankawa_people), [Kickapoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kickapoo), [Kiowa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiowa), [Tonkawa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tonkawa), and [Wichita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wichita_people).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERichardsonWintzBoswellAnderson202112-32][^cite_note-33] Many of these peoples migrated from the north or east during the colonial period, such as the [Choctaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Choctaw), Alabama-Coushatta, and [Delaware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lenape).[^cite_note-Carter_1995-27]&#x20;

The region was primarily controlled by the Spanish until the [Texas Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Revolution). They were most interested in relationships with the Caddo, who were - like the Spanish - a settled, agricultural people. Several Spanish missions were opened in Caddo territory, but a lack of interest in Christianity among the Caddo meant that few were converted. Positioned between French Louisiana and Spanish Texas, the Caddo maintained relations with both, but were closer with the French.[^cite_note-34] After Spain took control of Louisiana, most of the missions in eastern Texas were closed and abandoned.[^cite_note-35] The United States obtained Louisiana following the 1803 [Louisiana Purchase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louisiana_Purchase) and began convincing tribes to self-segregate from whites by moving west; facing an overflow of native peoples in Missouri and Arkansas, they were able to negotiate with the Caddo to allow several displaced peoples to settle on unused lands in eastern Texas. These included the [Muscogee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muscogee), [Houma Choctaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houma_people), [Lenape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lenape) and [Mingo Seneca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mingo), among others, who came to view the Caddoans as saviors.[^cite_note-Glover-36][^cite_note-37]&#x20;

The different temperaments of Native American tribes directly affected the fates of the European explorers and [settlers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Settler) coming to the area.[^cite_note-Richardson-2005-38] Friendly tribes taught newcomers how to grow local crops, prepare foods, and hunt [wild game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Game_\(food\)), while warlike tribes resisted the settlers.[^cite_note-Richardson-2005-38] Prior treaties with the Spanish forbade either side from militarizing its native population in any potential conflict between the two nations. Several outbreaks of violence between Native Americans and Texans started to spread in the prelude to the Texas Revolution. Texans accused tribes of stealing livestock.[^cite_note-Carter_1995-27] While no proof was found, those in charge of Texas at the time attempted to publicly blame and punish the Caddo, with speculation that the U.S. government was trying to keep them in check this way. The Caddo never turned to violence because of the situation, except in cases of self-defense.[^cite_note-Glover-36]&#x20;

By the 1830s, the U.S. had drafted the [Indian Removal Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Removal_Act), which was used to facilitate the [Trail of Tears](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trail_of_Tears). Fearing retribution, Indian Agents all over the eastern U.S. tried to convince all Indigenous peoples to uproot and move west. This included the Caddo of Louisiana and Arkansas. The first president of Texas, [Sam Houston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sam_Houston), aimed to cooperate and make peace with Native tribes, but his successor, [Mirabeau B. Lamar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mirabeau_B._Lamar), took a more hostile stance. Aggressive expansionism and systemic hostility by [Anglo-Texan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texians) settlers, sanctioned by state policy, compelled the forced relocation of most indigenous populations to federal lands north of the Red River, culminating in the establishment of [Indian Territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Territory) (present-day Oklahoma).[^cite_note-Carter_1995-27][^cite_note-Glover-36] Only the [Alabama-Coushatta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alabama%E2%80%93Coushatta_Tribe_of_Texas) would remain in the parts of Texas subject to white settlement, though the Comanche would continue to control most of the western half of the state until their defeat in the 1870s and 1880s.[^cite_note-39]&#x20;

### Colonization

Main articles: [New Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Spain), [New France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_France), [Louisiana (New France)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louisiana_\(New_France\)), [French colonization of Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_colonization_of_Texas), [Spanish Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Texas), [French and Indian War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_and_Indian_War), [Treaty of Paris (1763)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Paris_\(1763\)), [Seminole Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seminole_Wars), [Adams–Onís Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adams%E2%80%93On%C3%ADs_Treaty), [Mexican War of Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_War_of_Independence), [Treaty of Córdoba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_C%C3%B3rdoba), [First Mexican Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Mexican_Empire), [Mexican Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_Texas), [Provisional Government of Mexico (1823–24)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provisional_Government_of_Mexico_\(1823%E2%80%9324\)), [1824 Constitution of Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1824_Constitution_of_Mexico), [First Mexican Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Mexican_Republic), [Siete Leyes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siete_Leyes), and [Centralist Republic of Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centralist_Republic_of_Mexico)

The first historical document related to Texas was a map of the [Gulf Coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_Coast), created in 1519 by Spanish explorer [Alonso Álvarez de Pineda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alonso_%C3%81lvarez_de_Pineda).[^cite_note-40] Nine years later, shipwrecked Spanish explorer [Álvar Núñez Cabeza de Vaca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%81lvar_N%C3%BA%C3%B1ez_Cabeza_de_Vaca) and his cohort became the first Europeans in what is now Texas.[^cite_note-41][^cite_note-42] Cabeza de Vaca reported that in 1528, when the Spanish landed in the area, "half the natives died from a disease of the bowels and blamed us."[^cite_note-43] Cabeza de Vaca also made observations about the way of life of the Ignaces Natives of Texas.[^cite_note-45][^cite_note-46] [Francisco Vázquez de Coronado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_V%C3%A1zquez_de_Coronado) described another encounter with native people in 1541.[^cite_note-48][^cite_note-journeycoronado-47]&#x20;

The expedition of [Hernando de Soto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hernando_de_Soto) entered into Texas from the east, seeking a route to Mexico. They passed through the Caddo lands but turned back after reaching the River of Daycao (possibly the Brazos or Colorado), beyond which point the Native peoples were nomadic and did not have the agricultural stores to feed the expedition.[^cite_note-49][^cite_note-50]&#x20;

European powers ignored the area until accidentally settling there in 1685. Miscalculations by [René-Robert Cavelier de La Salle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ren%C3%A9-Robert_Cavelier_de_La_Salle) resulted in his establishing the colony of [Fort Saint Louis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_colonization_of_Texas) at [Matagorda Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matagorda_Bay) rather than along the [Mississippi River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mississippi_River).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeber1992149-51] The colony lasted only four years before succumbing to harsh conditions and hostile natives.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChipman199283-52] A small band of survivors traveled eastward into the lands of the Caddo, but La Salle was killed by disgruntled expedition members.[^cite_note-53]&#x20;

In 1690 Spanish authorities, concerned that France posed a competitive threat, constructed several [missions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_missions_in_Texas) in [East Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Texas) among the Caddo.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChipman199289-54] After Caddo resistance, the Spanish missionaries returned to Mexico.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeber1992155-55] When France began settling [Louisiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louisiana_\(New_France\)), in 1716 Spanish authorities responded by founding a new series of missions in East Texas.[^cite_note-56] Two years later, they created [San Antonio](53848.md) as the first Spanish civilian settlement in the area.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeber1992163-57]&#x20;



Nicolas de La Fora's 1771 map of the northern frontier of [New Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Spain) clearly shows the Provincia de los Tejas.

[^cite_note-58]

Hostile native tribes and distance from nearby Spanish colonies discouraged settlers from moving to the area. It was one of New Spain's least populated provinces.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChipman1992205-59] In 1749, the Spanish peace treaty with the [Lipan Apache](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lipan_Apache_people) angered many tribes,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeber1992193-60] including the Comanche, Tonkawa, and Hasinai.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeber1992189-61] The Comanche signed a treaty with Spain in 1785 and later helped to defeat the Lipan Apache and Karankawa tribes.[^cite_note-62][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeddle1995163-63] With numerous missions being established, priests led a peaceful conversion of most tribes. By the end of the 18th century only a few [nomadic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nomad) tribes had not converted.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChipman1992202-64]&#x20;



[Stephen F. Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_F._Austin) was the first American *[empresario](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empresario)* given permission to operate a colony within [Mexican Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_Texas).



Mexico in 1824. [Coahuila y Tejas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coahuila_y_Tejas) is the northeasternmost state.

When the United States [purchased Louisiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louisiana_Purchase) from France in 1803, American authorities insisted the agreement also included Texas. The boundary between New Spain and the United States was finally set in 1819 at the [Sabine River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabine_River_\(Texas%E2%80%93Louisiana\)), the modern border between Texas and Louisiana.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeber1992291–299-65] Eager for new land, many U.S. settlers refused to recognize the agreement. Several [filibusters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filibuster_\(military\)) raised armies to invade the area west of the Sabine River.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavis200646-66] Marked by the [War of 1812](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_of_1812), some men who had escaped from the Spanish, held (Old) Philippines had immigrated to and also passed through Texas (New Philippines)[^cite_note-67] and reached [Louisiana](18130.md) where [Philippine exiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_groups_in_the_Philippines) aided the United States in the defense of [New Orleans](53842.md) against a [British](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Empire) invasion, with [Filipinos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filipino_people) in the [Saint Malo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Malo,_Louisiana) settlement assisting [Jean Lafitte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean_Lafitte) in the [Battle of New Orleans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_New_Orleans).[^cite_note-68]&#x20;

In 1821, the [Mexican War of Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_War_of_Independence) included the Texas territory, which became part of Mexico.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeber1992300-69] Due to its low population, the territory was assigned to other [states and territories of Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_Mexico); the core territory was part of the state of [Coahuila y Tejas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coahuila_y_Tejas), but other parts of today's Texas were part of [Tamaulipas](222254.md), [Chihuahua](23962301.md), or the Mexican Territory of [Santa Fe de Nuevo México](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Fe_de_Nuevo_M%C3%A9xico).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEManchaca2001162-70]&#x20;

Hoping more settlers would reduce the near-constant Comanche raids, [Mexican Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_Texas) liberalized its immigration policies to permit immigrants from outside Mexico and Spain.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEManchaca2001164-71] As a result of this, large swathes of land were allotted to *[empresarios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empresario)*, who recruited settlers from the United States, Europe, and the Mexican interior, primarily the U.S. Austin's settlers, the [Old Three Hundred](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Three_Hundred), made places along the [Brazos River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazos_River) in 1822.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEManchaca2001198-72] The population of Texas grew rapidly. By 1834, the population had grown to about 37,800 people, with only 7,800 of Mexican descent.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEManchaca2001172,_201-73]&#x20;

Many immigrants openly flouted Mexican law, especially the prohibition against [slavery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavery). Combined with United States' attempts to purchase Texas, Mexican authorities decided in 1830 to prohibit continued immigration from the United States.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEdmondson200078-74] However, [illegal immigration](7124412.md) from the United States into Mexico continued to increase the population of Texas.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEManchaca2001200-75] New laws also called for the enforcement of [customs duties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duty_\(economics\)), angering native Mexican citizens (*[Tejanos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tejano)*) and recent immigrants alike.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavis200677-76] The [Anahuac Disturbances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anahuac_Disturbances) in 1832 were the first open revolt against Mexican rule, coinciding with a revolt in Mexico against the nation's president.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavis200685-77] [Texians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texian) sided with the [federalists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federalism) against the government and drove all Mexican soldiers out of East Texas.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavis200686–89-78] They took advantage of the lack of oversight to agitate for more political freedom. Texians met at the [Convention of 1832](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convention_of_1832) to discuss requesting independent statehood, among other issues.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavis200692-79] The following year, Texians reiterated their demands at the [Convention of 1833](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convention_of_1833).[^cite_note-80]&#x20;

### Republic

Main articles: [Texas Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Revolution), [Convention of 1836](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convention_of_1836), [Texas Declaration of Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Declaration_of_Independence), [Treaties of Velasco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaties_of_Velasco), and [Republic of Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Texas)

Within Mexico, tensions continued between federalists and centralists. In early 1835, wary [Texians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texians) formed Committees of Correspondence and Safety.[^cite_note-81] The unrest erupted into armed conflict in late 1835 at the [Battle of Gonzales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Gonzales).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHardin199412-82] This launched the [Texas Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Revolution). Texians elected delegates to the [Consultation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consultation_\(Texas\)), which created a provisional government.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWinders200472-83] The provisional government soon collapsed from infighting, and Texas was without clear governance for the first two months of 1836.[^cite_note-84]&#x20;



*Surrender of Santa Anna*. Painting by William Henry Huddle, 1886.

Mexican President [Antonio López de Santa Anna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_L%C3%B3pez_de_Santa_Anna) personally led an army to end the revolt.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHardin1994102-85] General [José de Urrea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_de_Urrea) defeated all the Texian resistance along the coast culminating in the [Goliad massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goliad_massacre).[^cite_note-86] López de Santa Anna's forces, after a [thirteen-day siege](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_the_Alamo), overwhelmed Texian defenders at the [Battle of the Alamo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_the_Alamo). News of the defeats sparked panic among Texas settlers.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETodishTodishSpring199868-87]&#x20;



The [Republic of Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Texas) with present-day borders superimposed

The newly elected Texian delegates to the [Convention of 1836](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convention_of_1836) quickly signed a [declaration of independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Declaration_of_Independence) on March 2, forming the [Republic of Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Texas). After electing interim officers, the Convention disbanded.[^cite_note-88] The new government joined the other settlers in Texas in the [Runaway Scrape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Runaway_Scrape), fleeing from the approaching Mexican army.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETodishTodishSpring199868-87]&#x20;

After several weeks of retreat, the [Texian Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texian_Army) commanded by [Sam Houston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sam_Houston) attacked and defeated López de Santa Anna's forces at the [Battle of San Jacinto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_San_Jacinto).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETodishTodishSpring199869-89] López de Santa Anna was captured and forced to sign the [Treaties of Velasco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaties_of_Velasco), ending the war.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETodishTodishSpring199870-90] The [Constitution of the Republic of Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_the_Republic_of_Texas) prohibited the government from restricting slavery or freeing slaves, and required free people of African descent to leave the country.[^cite_note-91]&#x20;

Political battles raged between two factions of the new Republic. The nationalist faction, led by [Mirabeau B. Lamar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mirabeau_B._Lamar), advocated the continued independence of Texas, the expulsion of the [Native Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_Americans_in_the_United_States), and the expansion of the Republic to the [Pacific Ocean](23070.md). Their opponents, led by Sam Houston, advocated the annexation of Texas to the United States and peaceful co-existence with Native Americans. The conflict between the factions was typified by an incident known as the [Texas Archive War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Archive_War).[^cite_note-92] With wide popular support, Texas first applied for annexation to the United States in 1836, but its status as a slaveholding country caused its admission to be controversial and it was initially rebuffed. This status, and Mexican diplomacy in support of its claims to the territory, also complicated Texas's ability to form foreign alliances and trade relationships.[^cite_note-seeds-93]&#x20;

The [Comanche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comanche) Indians furnished the main Native American opposition to the Texas Republic, manifested in multiple [raids on settlements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comanche_Wars).[^cite_note-94] Mexico launched two small expeditions into Texas in 1842. The town of San Antonio was captured twice and Texans were defeated in battle in the [Dawson massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dawson_massacre). Despite these successes, Mexico did not keep an occupying force in Texas, and the republic survived.[^cite_note-95] The cotton price crash of the 1840s depressed the country's economy.[^cite_note-seeds-93]&#x20;

### Statehood

Main article: [History of Texas (1845–1860)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Texas_\(1845%E2%80%931860\))

Further information: [Texas annexation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_annexation), [Admission to the Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Admission_to_the_Union), [Mexican–American War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican%E2%80%93American_War), and [Treaty of Guadalupe Hidalgo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Guadalupe_Hidalgo)

See also: [List of U.S. states by date of admission to the Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_by_date_of_admission_to_the_Union)



On March 2, 1936, the U.S. Post Office issued a [commemorative stamp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commemorative_stamp) commemorating the 100th anniversary of the Texas Declaration of Independence, featuring Sam Houston (left), Stephen Austin and the Alamo.

Texas was finally officially [annexed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_annexation) when the expansionist [James K. Polk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_K._Polk) won the [election of 1844](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1844_United_States_presidential_election).[^cite_note-96] On December 29, 1845, the [U.S. Congress](31756.md) admitted Texas to the U.S.[^cite_note-TAnnexation-97] After Texas's annexation, Mexico broke off diplomatic relations with the United States. While the United States claimed Texas's border stretched to the Rio Grande, Mexico claimed it was the [Nueces River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nueces_River) leaving the [Rio Grande Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_Grande_Valley_\(Texas\)) under contested Texan sovereignty.[^cite_note-TAnnexation-97] While the former Republic of Texas could not enforce its border claims, the United States had the military strength and the political will to do so. President Polk ordered General [Zachary Taylor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zachary_Taylor) south to the Rio Grande on January 13, 1846. A few months later Mexican troops routed an American cavalry patrol in the disputed area in the [Thornton Affair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thornton_Affair) starting the [Mexican–American War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican%E2%80%93American_War). The first battles of the war were fought in Texas: the [Siege of Fort Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Fort_Texas), [Battle of Palo Alto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Palo_Alto) and [Battle of Resaca de la Palma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Resaca_de_la_Palma). After these decisive victories, the United States invaded Mexican territory, ending the fighting in Texas.[^cite_note-Mwar-98]&#x20;



Captain [Charles A. May](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_A._May)'s squadron of the 2nd Dragoons slashes through the Mexican Army lines. [Resaca de la Palma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Resaca_de_la_Palma), Texas, May 1846.

The [Treaty of Guadalupe Hidalgo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Guadalupe_Hidalgo) ended the two-year war. In return for US\$18,250,000, (equivalent to \$679,111,000 in 2025), Mexico gave the U.S. undisputed control of Texas, ceded the [Mexican Cession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_Cession) in 1848, most of which today is called the American Southwest, and Texas's borders were established at the Rio Grande.[^cite_note-Mwar-98]&#x20;

The [Compromise of 1850](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compromise_of_1850) set Texas's boundaries at their present position: Texas ceded its claims to land which later became half of present-day [New Mexico](21649.md),[^cite_note-comp1850-99] a third of [Colorado](5399.md), and small portions of [Kansas](16716.md), [Oklahoma](22489.md), and [Wyoming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wyoming) to the federal government, in return for the assumption of \$10 million of the old republic's debt (equivalent to \$372,115,000 in 2025).[^cite_note-comp1850-99] Post-war Texas grew rapidly as migrants poured into the cotton lands of the state.[^cite_note-100] They also brought or purchased enslaved African Americans, whose numbers tripled in the state from 1850 to 1860, from 58,000 to 182,566.[^cite_note-101]&#x20;

### Civil War to late 19th century

Main article: [History of Texas (1865–1899)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Texas_\(1865%E2%80%931899\))

Further information: [Ordinance of Secession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ordinance_of_Secession), [Confederate States of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confederate_States_of_America), and [Texas in the American Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_in_the_American_Civil_War)

Texas re-entered war following the [election of 1860](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1860_United_States_presidential_election). During this time, Black people comprised 30 percent of the state's population, and they were overwhelmingly enslaved.[^cite_note-HOT-102] When [Abraham Lincoln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraham_Lincoln) was elected, South Carolina seceded from the Union; five other [Deep South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deep_South) states quickly followed. A state convention considering secession opened in Austin on January 28, 1861. On February 1, by a vote of 166–8, the convention adopted an [Ordinance of Secession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ordinance_of_Secession). Texas voters approved this Ordinance on February 23, 1861. Texas joined the newly created Confederate States of America on March 4, 1861, ratifying the permanent [C.S. Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_the_Confederate_States) on March 23.[^cite_note-facts-4][^cite_note-103]&#x20;

Not all Texans favored secession initially, although many of the same would later support the Southern cause. Texas's most notable [Unionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Unionist) was the state governor, [Sam Houston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sam_Houston). Not wanting to aggravate the situation, Houston refused two offers from President Lincoln for Union troops to keep him in office. After refusing to swear an oath of allegiance to the Confederacy, Houston was deposed.[^cite_note-104]&#x20;

While far from the [major battlefields](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Theater_of_the_American_Civil_War) of the [American Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Civil_War), Texas contributed large numbers of soldiers and equipment.[^cite_note-CivilwarHB-105] Union troops briefly [occupied](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Battle_of_Galveston) the state's primary port, Galveston. Texas's border with Mexico was known as the "backdoor of the Confederacy" because trade occurred at the border, bypassing the Union blockade.[^cite_note-106] The Confederacy repulsed all Union attempts to shut down this route,[^cite_note-CivilwarHB-105] but Texas's role as a supply state was marginalized in mid-1863 after the Union capture of the [Mississippi River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mississippi_River). The [final battle of the Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Palmito_Ranch) was fought at [Palmito Ranch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palmito_Ranch_Battlefield), near Brownsville, Texas, and saw a Confederate victory.[^cite_note-107][^cite_note-108]&#x20;

Texas descended into anarchy for two months between the [surrender](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Appomattox_Courthouse) of the [Army of Northern Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Army_of_Northern_Virginia) and the assumption of authority by Union General [Gordon Granger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gordon_Granger). Violence marked the early months of [Reconstruction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reconstruction_Era).[^cite_note-CivilwarHB-105] [Juneteenth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juneteenth) commemorates the announcement of the [Emancipation Proclamation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emancipation_Proclamation) in Galveston by General Gordon Granger, almost two and a half years after the original announcement.[^cite_note-109] President Johnson, in 1866, declared the civilian government restored in Texas.[^cite_note-110] Despite not meeting Reconstruction requirements, Congress resumed allowing elected Texas representatives into the federal government in 1870. Social volatility continued as the state struggled with agricultural depression and labor issues.[^cite_note-111]&#x20;

Like most of the South, the Texas economy was devastated by the War. However, since the state had not been as dependent on slaves as other parts of the South, it was able to recover more quickly. The culture in Texas during the later 19th century exhibited many facets of a frontier territory. The state became notorious as a haven for people from other parts of the country who wanted to escape debt, war tensions, or other problems. "Gone to Texas" was a common expression for those fleeing the law in other states. Nevertheless, the state also attracted many businessmen and other settlers with more legitimate interests.[^cite_note-112]&#x20;

The cattle industry continued to thrive, though it gradually became less profitable. Cotton and lumber became major industries creating new economic booms in various regions. Railroad networks grew rapidly as did the port at Galveston as commerce expanded. The lumber industry quickly expanded and was Texas's largest industry prior to the 20th century.[^cite_note-113]&#x20;

### Early to mid-20th century



Spindletop, the first major oil gusher

In 1900, Texas suffered the deadliest natural disaster in U.S. history in the form of the [Galveston hurricane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1900_Galveston_hurricane).[^cite_note-deadhurr-114] The fortunes of the state would turn around in 1901, with discovery of the first major [oil well](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_well) in Texas, on January 10th 1901. [Spindletop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spindletop), was found south of [Beaumont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beaumont,_Texas). Other fields were later discovered nearby in [East Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Texas_Oil_Field), [West Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Texas), and under the [Gulf of Mexico](21076367.md). The resulting "[oil boom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_oil_boom)" transformed Texas.[^cite_note-115] Oil production averaged three million barrels per day at its peak in 1972.[^cite_note-116]&#x20;

In 1901, the Democratic-dominated state legislature passed a bill requiring payment of a [poll tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poll_tax_\(United_States\)) for voting, which effectively [disenfranchised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disenfranchisement_after_the_Reconstruction_Era) most Black and many poor White and [Latino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latinos) people. In addition, the legislature established [white primaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_primaries), ensuring minorities were excluded from the formal political process. The number of voters dropped dramatically, and the Democrats crushed competition from the Republican and Populist parties.[^cite_note-117] The [Socialist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Party_of_Texas) became the second-largest party in Texas after 1912,[^cite_note-118] coinciding with a large socialist upsurge in the United States during fierce battles in the labor movement and the popularity of national heroes like [Eugene V. Debs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eugene_V._Debs). The socialists' popularity soon waned after their vilification by the federal government for their opposition to U.S. involvement in [World War I](4764461.md).[^cite_note-119][^cite_note-120]&#x20;

The [Great Depression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Depression) and the [Dust Bowl](59749.md) dealt a double blow to the state's economy, which had significantly improved since the Civil War. Migrants abandoned the worst-hit sections of Texas during the Dust Bowl years. Especially from this period on, Black people left Texas in the [Great Migration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Migration_\(African_American\)) to seek work in the Northern United States or California and to escape segregation.[^cite_note-HOT-102] In 1940, Texas was 74% [White](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Hispanic_Whites), 14.4% Black, and 11.5% Hispanic.[^cite_note-121]&#x20;

[World War II](32927.md) had a dramatic impact on Texas, as federal money poured in to build military bases, munitions factories, detention camps and Army hospitals; 750,000 Texans left for service; the cities exploded with new industry; and hundreds of thousands of poor farmers left the fields for service or much better-paying war jobs, never to return to agriculture.[^cite_note-122][^cite_note-123] Texas manufactured 3.1 percent of total United States military armaments produced during World War II, ranking eleventh among the 48 states.[^cite_note-124]&#x20;

Texas modernized and expanded its [system of higher education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Texas#Public_colleges_and_universities) through the 1960s. The state created a comprehensive plan for higher education, funded in large part by oil revenues, and a central state apparatus designed to manage state institutions more efficiently. These changes helped Texas universities receive federal research funds.[^cite_note-125]&#x20;

### Mid-20th to early 21st century

From around the mid-20th century, Texas began to transform from a rural and agricultural state to one urban and industrialized.[^cite_note-Calvert-2017-126] The state's population grew quickly during this period, with large levels of migration from outside the state.[^cite_note-Calvert-2017-126] As a part of the [Sun Belt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sun_Belt), Texas experienced strong economic growth, particularly during the 1970s and early 1980s.[^cite_note-Calvert-2017-126] Texas's economy diversified, lessening its reliance on the [petroleum industry](994887.md).[^cite_note-Calvert-2017-126] By 1990, [Hispanics and Latino Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_Americans) overtook Blacks to become the largest minority group.[^cite_note-Calvert-2017-126] Texas has the largest Black population with over 3.9 million.[^cite_note-127]&#x20;

During the late 20th century, the [Republican Party](32070.md) replaced the [Democratic Party](5043544.md) as the dominant party in the state.[^cite_note-Calvert-2017-126] Beginning in the early 21st century, metropolitan areas including [Dallas–Fort Worth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dallas%E2%80%93Fort_Worth_metroplex) and [Greater Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Austin) became centers for the [Texas Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Democratic_Party) in statewide and national elections as liberal policies became more accepted in urban areas.[^cite_note-City_Journal-2016-128][^cite_note-129][^cite_note-130][^cite_note-131]&#x20;

From the mid-2000s to 2019, Texas gained an influx of business relocations and regional headquarters from companies in [California](5407.md).[^cite_note-132][^cite_note-133][^cite_note-134][^cite_note-135] Texas became a major destination for migration during the early 21st century and was named the most popular state to move for three consecutive years.[^cite_note-136] Another study in 2019 determined Texas's growth rate at 1,000 people per day.[^cite_note-137] During the [COVID-19 pandemic in Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Texas), Texas was one of the states to resist the imposition of repeated lockdowns.[^cite_note-138][^cite_note-139]&#x20;

During February 13–17, 2021, the state faced a major weather emergency as [Winter Storm Uri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winter_Storm_Uri) hit the state, as well as most of the Southeastern and Midwestern United States.[^cite_note-140][^cite_note-141] Historically high power usage across the state caused the state's power grid to become overworked and [ERCOT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ERCOT) (the main operator of the [Texas Interconnection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Interconnection) grid) declared an emergency and began to implement rolling blackouts across Texas, causing a [power crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Texas_power_crisis).[^cite_note-142][^cite_note-143][^cite_note-144] Over 3 million Texans were without power and over 4 million were under boil-water notices.[^cite_note-145]&#x20;

## Geography

Main article: [Geography of Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Texas)



[Sam Rayburn Reservoir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sam_Rayburn_Reservoir)



[Texas Hill Country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Hill_Country)

Texas is the [second-largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_and_territories_by_area) U.S. state by area, after [Alaska](624.md), and the largest state within the [contiguous United States](83759.md), at 268,820 square miles (696,200 km<sup>2</sup>). If it were an independent country, Texas would be the [39th-largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_area).[^cite_note-146] It ranks 26th worldwide amongst [country subdivisions by size](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_the_largest_country_subdivisions_by_area).&#x20;

Texas is in the [south central](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Central_United_States) part of the United States. The [Rio Grande](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_Grande) forms a natural border with the Mexican states of [Chihuahua](23962301.md), [Coahuila](160592.md), [Nuevo León](222247.md), and [Tamaulipas](222254.md) to the south. The [Red River](194242.md) forms a natural border with Oklahoma and Arkansas to the north. The [Sabine River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabine_River_\(Texas-Louisiana\)) forms a natural border with Louisiana to the east. The [Texas Panhandle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Panhandle) has an eastern border with Oklahoma at [100° W](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/100th_meridian_west), a northern border with Oklahoma at [36°30' N](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parallel_36%C2%B030%27_north) and a western border with New Mexico at [103° W](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/103rd_meridian_west). [El Paso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Paso) lies on the state's western tip at [32° N](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/32nd_parallel_north) and the Rio Grande.[^cite_note-comp1850-99]&#x20;

Texas has 10 [climatic regions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification), 14 [soil regions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agricultural_soil_science) and 11 distinct [ecological regions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecoregion).[^cite_note-147] One classification system divides Texas, in order from southeast to west, into the following: [Gulf Coastal Plains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_Coastal_Plains), Interior Lowlands, [Great Plains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Plains), and Basin and Range Province.[^cite_note-Physical_Regions_of_Texas-148]&#x20;

The Gulf Coastal Plains region wraps around the Gulf of Mexico on the southeast section of the state. Vegetation in this region consists of thick piney woods. The Interior Lowlands region consists of gently rolling to hilly forested land and is part of a larger pine-hardwood forest. The [Cross Timbers region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cross_Timbers) and [Caprock Escarpment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caprock_Escarpment) are part of the Interior Lowlands.[^cite_note-Physical_Regions_of_Texas-148]&#x20;



[Steinhagen Reservoir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steinhagen_Reservoir)

The Great Plains region in Central Texas spans through the state's [panhandle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Panhandle) and [Llano Estacado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Llano_Estacado) to the state's [hill country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Hill_Country) near [Lago Vista](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lago_Vista,_Texas) and [Austin](1998.md). This region is dominated by [prairie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prairie) and [steppe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steppe). "Far West Texas" or the "[Trans-Pecos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trans-Pecos)" region is the state's Basin and Range Province. The most varied of the regions, this area includes Sand Hills, the [Stockton Plateau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edwards_Plateau), desert valleys, wooded mountain slopes and desert grasslands.[^cite_note-149]&#x20;

Texas has 3,700 named streams and 15 major rivers,[^cite_note-150][^cite_note-151] with the [Rio Grande](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_Grande) as the largest. Other major rivers include the [Pecos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pecos_River), the [Brazos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazos_River), [Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_River_\(Texas\)), and [Red River](194242.md). While Texas has few natural lakes, Texans have built more than a hundred [artificial reservoirs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_lakes_in_Texas).[^cite_note-152]&#x20;

The size and unique history of Texas make its regional affiliation debatable; it can be considered a Southern or a Southwestern state, or both. The vast geographic, economic, and cultural diversity within the state itself prohibits easy categorization of the whole state into a [recognized region of the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_regions_of_the_United_States). Notable extremes range from [East Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Texas) which is often considered an extension of the [Deep South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deep_South), to [Far West Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trans-Pecos) which is generally acknowledged to be part of the [interior Southwest](179571.md).[^cite_note-153]&#x20;

### Geology

Main article: [Geology of Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geology_of_Texas)



[Palo Duro Canyon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palo_Duro_Canyon)



[Franklin Mountains State Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin_Mountains_State_Park)



[Big Bend National Park](306829.md)

Texas is the southernmost part of the Great Plains, which ends in the south against the folded [Sierra Madre Occidental](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sierra_Madre_Occidental) of Mexico. The [continental crust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continental_crust) forms a stable [Mesoproterozoic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mesoproterozoic) [craton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Craton) which changes across a broad continental margin and transitional crust into true [oceanic crust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oceanic_crust) of the Gulf of Mexico. The oldest rocks in Texas date from the Mesoproterozoic and are about 1,600 million years old.[^cite_note-154]&#x20;

The margin created by the continental crust existed until [Laurasia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laurasia) and [Gondwana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gondwana) collided in the [Pennsylvanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvanian_\(geology\)) subperiod to form [Pangea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pangea).[^cite_note-155] Pangea began to break up in the [Triassic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Triassic), but [seafloor spreading](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seafloor_spreading) to form the Gulf of Mexico occurred only in the mid- and [late Jurassic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late_Jurassic). The shoreline shifted again to the eastern margin of the state and the Gulf of Mexico's passive margin began to form. Today 9 to 12 miles (14 to 19 km) of sediments are buried beneath the Texas continental shelf and a large proportion of remaining US [oil reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_reserves) are here. The incipient Gulf of Mexico basin was restricted and seawater often evaporated completely to form thick [evaporite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evaporite) deposits of Jurassic age. These salt deposits formed [salt dome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salt_dome) [diapirs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diapir), and are found in East Texas along the Gulf coast.[^cite_note-156]&#x20;

East Texas outcrops consist of [Cretaceous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cretaceous) and [Paleogene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paleogene) sediments which contain important deposits of [Eocene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eocene) [lignite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lignite). The Mississippian and Pennsylvanian sediments in the north; Permian sediments in the west; and Cretaceous sediments in the east, along the Gulf coast and out on the Texas [continental shelf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continental_shelf) contain oil. [Oligocene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oligocene) [volcanic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volcanic) rocks are found in far west Texas in the [Big Bend](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Bend,_Texas) area. A blanket of [Miocene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miocene) sediments known as the [Ogallala formation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ogallala_Aquifer) in the western high plains region is an important [aquifer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aquifer).[^cite_note-157] Located far from an active [plate tectonic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plate_tectonic) boundary, Texas has no [volcanoes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volcanoes) and few earthquakes.[^cite_note-158]&#x20;

### Wildlife

See also: [List of mammals of Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_mammals_of_Texas), [List of birds of Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_birds_of_Texas), [List of reptiles of Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_reptiles_of_Texas), and [List of amphibians of Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_amphibians_of_Texas)

Texas is home to 65 species of mammals, 213 species of reptiles and amphibians, including the [American green tree frog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_green_tree_frog), and the greatest diversity of bird life in the United States—590 native species in all.[^cite_note-159] At least 12 species have been introduced and now reproduce freely in Texas.[^cite_note-160]&#x20;

Texas plays host to several species of [wasps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wasp), including an abundance of *[Polistes exclamans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polistes_exclamans)*,[^cite_note-161] and is an important ground for the study of *[Polistes annularis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polistes_annularis)*.[^cite_note-162]&#x20;

During the spring Texas [wildflowers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wildflowers) such as the state flower, the [bluebonnet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lupinus_texensis), line highways throughout Texas. During the Johnson Administration the first lady, [Lady Bird Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lady_Bird_Johnson), worked to draw attention to Texas wildflowers.[^cite_note-163]&#x20;

### Climate

Main article: [Climate of Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_of_Texas)



Köppen climate types in Texas

The large size of Texas and its location at the intersection of multiple [climate zones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification) gives the state highly variable weather. The [Panhandle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Panhandle) of the state has colder winters than North Texas, while the Gulf Coast has mild winters. Texas has wide variations in precipitation patterns. El Paso, on the western end of the state, averages 8.7 inches (220 mm) of annual rainfall,[^cite_note-164] while parts of southeast Texas average as much as 64 inches (1,600 mm) per year.[^cite_note-165] Dallas in the North Central region averages a more moderate 37 inches (940 mm) per year.[^cite_note-166]&#x20;

Snow falls multiple times each winter in the Panhandle and mountainous areas of West Texas, once or twice a year in North Texas, and once every few years in Central and East Texas. Snow falls south of San Antonio or on the coast only in rare circumstances. Of note is the [2004 Christmas Eve snowstorm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_Christmas_Eve_snowstorm), when 6 inches (150 mm) of snow fell as far south as [Kingsville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingsville,_Texas), where the average high temperature in December is 65 °F.[^cite_note-167]&#x20;

Night-time summer temperatures range from the upper 50s °F (14 °C) in the West Texas mountains to 80 °F (27 °C) in Galveston.[^cite_note-168][^cite_note-169]&#x20;

The table below consists of averages for August (generally the warmest month) and January (generally the coldest) in selected cities in various regions of the state.&#x20;

| Location | August (°F) | August (°C) | January (°F) | January (°C) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [Houston](13774.md) | 94/75 | 34/24 | 63/54 | 17/12 |
| [San Antonio](53848.md) | 96/74 | 35/23 | 63/40 | 17/5 |
| [Dallas](53838.md) | 96/77 | 36/25 | 57/37 | 16/3 |
| [Austin](1998.md) | 97/74 | 36/23 | 61/45 | 16/5 |
| [El Paso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Paso,_Texas) | 92/67 | 33/21 | 57/32 | 14/0 |
| [Laredo](136773.md) | 100/77 | 37/25 | 67/46 | 19/7 |
| [Amarillo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amarillo,_Texas) | 89/64 | 32/18 | 50/23 | 10/−4 |
| [Brownsville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brownsville,_Texas) | 94/76 | 34/24 | 70/51 | 21/11 |

#### Storms

See also: [List of Texas hurricanes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Texas_hurricanes)

[Thunderstorms](70807.md) strike Texas often, especially the eastern and northern portions of the state. [Tornado Alley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tornado_Alley) covers the northern section of Texas. The state experiences the most [tornadoes](37530.md) in the United States, an average of 139 a year. These strike most frequently in North Texas and the Panhandle.[^cite_note-171] Tornadoes in Texas generally occur in April, May, and June.[^cite_note-172]&#x20;

Some of the most destructive hurricanes in U.S. history have seriously impacted Texas. A hurricane in 1875 killed about 400 people in [Indianola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indianola,_Texas), followed by [another hurricane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1886_Indianola_hurricane) in 1886 that destroyed the town. These events meant that [Galveston](53840.md) took over as the chief port city. The [1900 Galveston hurricane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1900_Galveston_hurricane) subsequently devastated that city, killing a number estimated at from 8,000 to 12,000 in the deadliest [natural disaster](58921.md) in U.S. history.[^cite_note-deadhurr-114] In 2017, [Hurricane Harvey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane_Harvey) caused a landfall in [Rockport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rockport,_Texas) as a Category 4 Hurricane, causing significant damage, with its unprecedented amounts of rain over the [Greater Houston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Houston) area resulting in widespread and catastrophic flooding, affecting hundreds of thousands of homes. Harvey ultimately became the costliest hurricane worldwide, causing an estimated \$198.6 billion in damage, surpassing the cost of [Hurricane Katrina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane_Katrina).[^cite_note-173]&#x20;

Other devastating Texas hurricanes include the [1915 Galveston hurricane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1915_Galveston_hurricane), [Hurricane Audrey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane_Audrey) in 1957, [Hurricane Carla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane_Carla) in 1961, [Hurricane Beulah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane_Beulah) in 1967, [Hurricane Alicia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane_Alicia) in 1983, [Hurricane Rita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane_Rita) in 2005, and [Hurricane Ike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane_Ike) in 2008. [Tropical storms](8282374.md) have also caused their share of damage: [Allison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_Storm_Allison_\(1989\)) in 1989 and again [during 2001](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_Storm_Allison), [Claudette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_Storm_Claudette_\(1979\)) in 1979, and [Tropical Storm Imelda in 2019.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_Storm_Imelda)[^cite_note-174][^cite_note-175][^cite_note-176]&#x20;

There is no substantial physical barrier between Texas and the [polar region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polar_regions_of_Earth), and although rare, it is possible for arctic or polar [air masses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_mass) to penetrate Texas,[^cite_note-177][^cite_note-178] as occurred during the [February 13–17, 2021 North American winter storm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/February_13%E2%80%9317,_2021_North_American_winter_storm).[^cite_note-179][^cite_note-180] Usually, prevailing winds in North America push polar air masses to the southeast before they reach Texas. Because such incidents are rare, and, generally unexpected, they may result in crises such as the [2021 Texas power crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Texas_power_crisis).&#x20;

### Greenhouse gases

Main article: [Climate change in Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_in_Texas)

As of 2017, Texas emitted the most [greenhouse gases](21350772.md) in the U.S.[^cite_note-eia17-181] As of 2017 the state emits about 1,600 billion pounds (707 million metric tons) of carbon dioxide annually.[^cite_note-eia17-181] As an independent state, Texas would rank as the world's seventh-largest producer of greenhouse gases.[^cite_note-GH2-182] Causes of the state's vast greenhouse gas emissions include the state's large number of [coal power plants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fossil_fuel_power_plant) and the state's refining and manufacturing industries.[^cite_note-GH2-182] In 2010, there were 2,553 "emission events" which poured 44.6 million pounds (20,200 metric tons) of contaminants into the Texas sky.[^cite_note-183]&#x20;

### Administrative divisions

See also: [List of counties in Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_counties_in_Texas), [List of Texas metropolitan areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Texas_metropolitan_areas), and [List of municipalities in Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_municipalities_in_Texas)

| [Largest city in Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_municipalities_in_Texas) by year[^cite_note-Census_Largest_Cities-184] |  |
| --- | --- |
| Year(s) | City |
| 1850–1870 | [San Antonio](53848.md)[^cite_note-SATX_TSHA-185] |
| 1870–1890 | [Galveston](53840.md)[^cite_note-186] |
| 1890–1900 | [Dallas](53838.md)[^cite_note-Census_Largest_Cities-184] |
| 1900–1930 | [San Antonio](53848.md)[^cite_note-SATX_TSHA-185] |
| 1930–present | [Houston](13774.md)[^cite_note-187] |



[Texas Counties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_counties_in_Texas) and [municipalities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_municipalities_in_Texas)



[Colonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonia_\(United_States\)) in the [Rio Grande Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_Grande_Valley_\(Texas\)) near the [Mexico–United States border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexico%E2%80%93United_States_border)

The state has three cities with populations exceeding one million: Houston, San Antonio, and Dallas.[^cite_note-188] These three rank among the 10 most populous cities of the United States. As of 2020, six Texas cities had populations greater than 600,000. Austin, Fort Worth, and El Paso are among the 20 [largest U.S. cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_States_cities_by_population). Texas has four [metropolitan areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Texas_metropolitan_areas) with populations greater than a million: [Dallas–Fort Worth–Arlington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dallas%E2%80%93Fort_Worth_Metroplex), [Houston–Sugar Land–The Woodlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Houston), [San Antonio–New Braunfels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_San_Antonio), and [Austin–Round Rock–San Marcos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Austin). The Dallas–Fort Worth and Houston metropolitan areas number about 7.5 million and 7 million residents as of 2019, respectively.[^cite_note-189]&#x20;

Three [interstate highways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_Highway_System)—[I-35](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_35_\(Texas\)) to the west (Dallas–Fort Worth to San Antonio, with Austin in between), [I-45](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_45_\(Texas\)) to the east (Dallas to Houston), and [I-10](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_10_\(Texas\)) to the south (San Antonio to Houston) define the [Texas Urban Triangle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Urban_Triangle) region. The region of 60,000 square miles (160,000 km<sup>2</sup>) contains most of the state's largest cities and metropolitan areas as well as 17 million people, nearly 75 percent of Texas's total population.[^cite_note-190] Houston and Dallas have been recognized as [world cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_city).[^cite_note-191] These cities are spread out amongst the state.[^cite_note-192]&#x20;

In contrast to the cities, unincorporated rural settlements known as [colonias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonia_\(border_settlement\)) often lack basic infrastructure and are marked by poverty.[^cite_note-Colonias-193] The office of the Texas Attorney General stated, in 2011, that Texas had about 2,294 colonias, and estimates about 500,000 lived in the colonias. [Hidalgo County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hidalgo_County,_Texas), as of 2011, has the largest number of colonias.[^cite_note-194] Texas has the largest number of people living in [colonias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonia_\(United_States\)) of all states.[^cite_note-Colonias-193]&#x20;

Texas has [254 counties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_counties_in_Texas), more than any other state.[^cite_note-195] Each county runs on [Commissioners' Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commissioners%27_Court) system consisting of four elected commissioners (one from each of four precincts in the county, roughly divided according to population) and a county judge elected at large from the entire county. County government runs similar to a "weak" [mayor-council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor-council) system; the county judge has no veto authority, but votes along with the other commissioners.[^cite_note-196][^cite_note-auto-197]&#x20;

Although Texas permits cities and counties to enter "interlocal agreements" to share services, the state does not allow [consolidated city-county](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consolidated_city-county) governments, nor does it have [metropolitan governments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_government). Counties are not granted [home rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Home_rule) status; their powers are strictly defined by state law.[^cite_note-198][^cite_note-auto-197] The state does not have townships—areas within a county are either incorporated or unincorporated. Incorporated areas are part of a municipality. The county provides limited services to unincorporated areas and to some smaller incorporated areas. Municipalities are classified either "general law" cities or "home rule".[^cite_note-199] A municipality may elect home rule status once it exceeds 5,000 population with voter approval.[^cite_note-200]&#x20;

Texas also permits the creation of "special districts", which provide limited services. The most common is the [school district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/School_district), but can also include hospital districts, community college districts, and utility districts. Municipal, school district, and special district elections are [nonpartisan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-partisan_democracy),[^cite_note-201] though the party affiliation of a candidate may be well-known. County and state elections are partisan.[^cite_note-202]&#x20;

Texas population density map

The resident population of Texas was 29,145,505 in the [2020 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_United_States_census), a 15.9% increase since the [2010 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_United_States_census).[^cite_note-Historical_Population_Change_Data-204] At the 2020 census, the apportioned population of Texas stood at 29,183,290.[^cite_note-205] The [U.S. Census Bureau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Census_Bureau) estimated the population was 31,290,831 as of July 1, 2024, an increase of 7.4% since the [2020 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_United_States_census).[^cite_note-CensusQuickFacts-1] Texas is the second-most populous state in the United States after California and the only other U.S. state to surpass a total estimated population of 30 million people as of July 2, 2022.[^cite_note-206][^cite_note-207]&#x20;

In 2015, Texas had 4.7 million foreign-born residents, about 17% of the population and 21.6% of the state workforce.[^cite_note-AICTexas-208] The major countries of origin for Texan immigrants were Mexico (55.1% of immigrants), India (5%), El Salvador (4.3%), Vietnam (3.7%), and China (2.3%).[^cite_note-AICTexas-208] Of immigrant residents, 35.8 percent were [naturalized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naturalization) U.S. citizens.[^cite_note-AICTexas-208] As of 2018, the population increased to 4.9 million foreign-born residents or 17.2% of the state population, up from 2,899,642 in 2000.[^cite_note-209]&#x20;

In 2014, there were an estimated 1.7 million undocumented immigrants in Texas, making up 35% of the total Texas immigrant population and 6.1% of the total state population.[^cite_note-AICTexas-208] In addition to the state's foreign-born population, 4.1 million Texans (15% of the state's population) were born in the United States yet had at least one immigrant parent.[^cite_note-AICTexas-208] According to the [American Community Survey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Community_Survey)'s 2019 estimates, 1,739,000 residents were undocumented immigrants, a decrease of 103,000 since 2014 but an increase of 142,000 since 2016. Of the undocumented immigrant population, 951,000 had resided in Texas no more than 14 years; an estimated 788,000 had lived in Texas for 15 or more years.[^cite_note-210]&#x20;

Texas's [Rio Grande Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_Grande_Valley_\(Texas\)) has seen significant migration from across the [U.S.–Mexico border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S.%E2%80%93Mexico_border). During the [2014 crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_American_immigration_crisis), many [Central Americans](6121.md), including unaccompanied minors traveling alone from [Guatemala](17238567.md), [Honduras](13394.md), and [El Salvador](9356.md), reached the state, overwhelming Border Patrol resources for a time. Many sought [asylum](1621432.md) in the United States.[^cite_note-211][^cite_note-212]&#x20;

Texas's population density as of 2020 is 110 people per square mile (42 people/km<sup>2</sup>) which is slightly higher than the average [population density](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_population_density) of the U.S. as a whole, at 93 people per square mile (36 people/km<sup>2</sup>). In contrast, while Texas and France are similarly sized geographically, the European country has a population density of 256 people per square mile (99 people/km<sup>2</sup>). Using [population-weighted density](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population-weighted_density)[^cite_note-213], Texas has a density of 3,604 people per square mile (1,392 people/km<sup>2</sup>), lower than the U.S. average, at 5,217 people per square mile (2,014 people/km<sup>2</sup>)[^cite_note-214][^cite_note-215]. Two-thirds of all Texans live in major metropolitan areas such as Houston.&#x20;

According to [HUD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Housing_and_Urban_Development)'s 2022 [Annual Homeless Assessment Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annual_Homeless_Assessment_Report_to_Congress), there were an estimated 24,432 [homeless](19394651.md) people in Texas.[^cite_note-216][^cite_note-217]&#x20;

[^cite_note-223]

In 2019, [non-Hispanic Whites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Hispanic_or_Latino_whites) represented 41.2% of Texas's population, reflecting a national demographic shift.[^cite_note-224][^cite_note-225][^cite_note-226] [Black people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_Americans_in_Texas) made up 12.9%, [American Indians and Alaska Natives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_Americans_in_Texas) 1.0%, [Asian Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_Americans) 5.2%, [Native Hawaiians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_Hawaiians) and other [Pacific Islanders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Islander_Americans) 0.1%, some other race 0.2%, and [two or more races](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiracial_Americans) 1.8%. [Hispanics or Latino Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanics_and_Latinos_in_Texas) of any race made up 39.7% of the estimated population.[^cite_note-227]&#x20;

At the 2020 census, the racial and ethnic composition of the state was 42.5% [White](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Americans) (39.8% non-Hispanic White), 11.8% Black, 5.4% Asian, 0.3% American Indian and Alaska Native, 0.1% Native Hawaiian and other Pacific Islander, 13.6% some other race, 17.6% two or more races, and 40.2% Hispanic and Latino American of any race. Minority Americans make up 60.2% of the Texas population.[^cite_note-228][^cite_note-229]&#x20;

In 2010, 49% of all births were Hispanics; 35% were non-Hispanic White; 11.5% were non-Hispanic Black, and 4.3% were Asians/Pacific Islanders.[^cite_note-230] Based on U.S. Census Bureau data released in February 2011, for the first time in recent history, Texas's White population is below 50% (45%) and Hispanics grew to 38%. Between 2000 and 2010, the total population grew by 20.6%, but Hispanics and Latino Americans grew by 65%, whereas non-Hispanic Whites grew by only 4.2%.[^cite_note-231] Texas has the fifth highest rate of teenage births in the nation and a plurality of these are to Hispanics or Latinos.[^cite_note-232][^cite_note-233] As of 2022, Hispanics and Latinos of any race replaced the non-Hispanic White population as the largest share of the state's population.[^cite_note-234]&#x20;

Texas has the second-largest share of [Mexican Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_Americans) in the U.S., making up 32.2% of the total population and 80% of the state's Hispanic population.[^cite_note-censusreporter.org-235] Other than Mexican, the largest self-reported ancestries in the state as of 2022 were [German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Americans) (8.1%), [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_Americans) (7.9%), [Irish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irish_Americans) (5.8%), those identifying as [American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_ancestry) (4.6%), [Italian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Americans) (1.9%), [Indian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Americans) (1.9%), [Salvadoran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salvadoran_Americans) (1.4%), [Scottish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scottish_Americans) (1.3%), [Vietnamese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_Americans) (1.1%), [Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Americans) (1%), [Puerto Rican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puerto_Ricans) (0.9%), [Polish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Americans) (0.9%), [Honduran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honduran_Americans) (0.8%), [Filipino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filipino_Americans) (0.8%), and [Scotch-Irish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scotch-Irish_Americans) (0.7%).[^cite_note-236][^cite_note-237][^cite_note-censusreporter.org-235]&#x20;
